# **STEP 3 — Convert Act Beats → Episode Beats (Books 7–9 first)**  
We expand the **Loom Trilogy** first because:

1. Loom has the largest number of cross-system constraints (Mechanica v4, Symbolic Action Engine, Ascendants, VT/LT conversions, etc.)  
2. It is the easiest to check continuity against the Saga Endgame Canon (Book 9 Mending logic).  
3. Completing 7–9 provides the forward shape that 1–6 must weld into.

### Output:
For Books 7–9, each episode gets:

- ECID  
- ~3–6 beats (variable)  
- Beat IDs (BIDs)  
- Mode tags (ROM/HUM/ACTION/SUPP)  
- Heat tags (H0–H4, when allowed)  
- Symbol/Motif selection  
- Resonance-state tag (CALM/BLOOM/SHARD/RUPTURE/NODE/VT/LT)

These become the **true saga beats**.

# ✅ **HOW WE HANDLE PLACEHOLDERS**
For each book:

1. **Determine target number** (e.g., Book 1 → 65 episodes)  
2. **Multiply across the three acts proportionally**  
   - But acts can flex: 18–24 episodes Act I, 18–26 Act II, 20–30 Act III  
3. Insert episode placeholders:

Example (Book 1):

```
S1.T1.B01.A01.Ep01 — Placeholder
S1.T1.B01.A01.Ep02 — Placeholder
...
S1.T1.B01.A01.Ep22 — Act I placeholder end

S1.T1.B01.A02.Ep23 — Placeholder
...
S1.T1.B01.A02.Ep43 — Act II placeholder end

S1.T1.B01.A03.Ep44 — Placeholder
...
S1.T1.B01.A03.Ep65 — Book 1 final episode placeholder
```

4. Overlay the **act-level containers** (the ones we already built) onto appropriate placeholder slots.  
5. During beat expansion, we “fill in” these placeholders into full episode structures.

# ✅ **Your Question: Should we audit against project memory before or after the placeholders?**

## **Short Answer:**  
**Audit AFTER the placeholders**, not before.

## **Long Answer (why auditing before is risky):**

When we audit, we check the following systems:
- Mechanica v4 (resonance physics, UARS cost, corridor/weather)
- Symbol/Motif System (geometry, color, VFX drift rules)
- Romance/Heat system (H0–H4, Rung progression)
- Fun Layer cadence rules (one every 5–8 episodes)
- Antagonist-pressure layering (Dominions → Hardliners → Choirless → Collapse → Brightbreak)
- POV distribution (30/30/30/10)
- Episode/Scene Workflow constraints (symbolic consequence, beat-output rules)

These systems **attach to individual beats and episodes**, not to the act-level containers.

Right now, our episode list is **incomplete**, which means an audit now would:

- Pass incorrectly where pressure hasn’t been placed yet,  
- Fail to catch issues because episodes don’t exist yet,  
- Produce noise rather than diagnostic clarity.

### Auditing *before* placeholders = guaranteed rework.  
### Auditing *after* placeholders = clean, stable, accurate.

---

# 🧠 **Correct pipeline order (to avoid drift + rework)**

### **PHASE 1 (done)** — Saga Grid at act-level  
### **PHASE 2 (next)** — Inflate Grid to full episode placeholders (600–720 episodes)  
### **PHASE 3** — Apply SIDs to every episode  
### **PHASE 4** — Fill act-level episode containers into the placeholder grid

#### ✓ Mechanica audit  
#### ✓ VFX/Symbol audit  
#### ✓ Romance + Heat audit  
#### ✓ Character arcs / POV audit  
#### ✓ Antagonist-pressure audit  
#### ✓ FUN cadence audit  

### **PHASE 7** — Corrections + alignment  
### **PHASE 8** — Final Beat Bible delivery for each trilogy

## ASSISTANT

Great questions — and you’re right to pause here.

---

# ✅ **SHORT ANSWER**
**Do NOT build all detailed beats before auditing.  
Do NOT audit act-level beats before building placeholders either.**

The **optimal path** is:

### **1. Expand to placeholder episodes** (structure only)  
### **2. Weld the act-level containers onto those placeholders**  
### **3. AUDIT this intermediate grid (structural audit)**  
### **4. THEN expand detailed beats** using the audited structure  
### **5. Run the full memory audits AFTER detailed beats exist**

This gives the project **maximal stability**, minimal rework, and zero drift.

---

# 🧠 **WHY WE DO NOT AUDIT AFTER FULL BEAT EXPANSION**
If we expand all beats first (600–900 beats across 600+ episodes):

- You expose yourself to **massive rework**  
- Any structural change (episode move, POV shift, environment correction) propagates across dozens or hundreds of beats  
- The audit would identify errors deeply baked into the beat text that require rewriting

This is the equivalent of **framing the entire house before verifying the foundation is level**.

---

# 🧠 **WHY WE DO NOT AUDIT NOW (at act-level)**
Right now, beats are:

- Very large-grain  
- Missing episode locality  
- Missing environment (ECID) attachments  
- Missing POV distributions  
- Missing corridor/weather conditions  
- Missing antagonist-pressure curves at episode level  
- Missing romance heat gates  
- Missing symbol constraints  
- Missing UARS cost points  
- Missing FUN cadence

Act-level beats **cannot be meaningfully audited** because the systems in project memory operate at the **episode + beat granularity**, not the act granularity.

Auditing now would produce nothing actionable.

---

# 🧭 **THE TRUE OPTIMAL PATH (step-by-step)**  
This is the cleanest, fastest, least risky workflow for a saga of this complexity.

---

## **PHASE 1 — Build placeholder episode grid**  
✔ 60–80 episodes per book  
✔ 20–22 episodes in Act I  
✔ 22–26 in Act II  
✔ 20–25 in Act III  
✔ SIDs assigned  
✔ Zero beat detail yet  
This gives us the **scaffolding**.

---

## **PHASE 2 — Weld existing act-level beats onto placeholder episodes**
We take your act-level beats, and place them as **anchor nodes** inside the placeholder grid.

Example:

- Act-level beat “Seraphine fails to save the swamp child” becomes Episode 1  
- “Lucien misalignment #1” becomes Episode 3  
- “Pattern accelerates” becomes Episode 13  
- etc.

This step produces:

### **Mid-resolution Saga Beat Spine**  
This is the first point where:

- All episode boundaries exist  
- All acts are paced  
- All major events have their place  
- POV ratios for each act become visible  
- Corridor & environment mapping becomes coherent  
- Romance ladder/heat rung timing becomes visible  
- Antagonist vectors align with the correct episode windows

This is the **first moment where a real audit becomes useful**.

---

## **PHASE 3 — Structural Audit USING PROJECT MEMORY**
This is NOT a detailed beat audit.  
This is a **continuity and topology audit**.

We run:

### Structural / Environmental Audit
- Corridor pressure  
- Weather escalation  
- City dialect consistency  
- Travel timing  
- Node directionality  
- Resonance amplitude by era

### Antagonist Pressure Audit
- Dominion pressure in Veil  
- Technarch → Choirless escalation in Neon  
- Brightbreak infiltration in Loom

### POV Ratio Audit
30% Saga Lead  
30% Trilogy Lead  
30% Support  
10% Flex

### FUN Cadence Audit
At least 1 fun beat per 5–8 episodes.

### Heat & Romance Stage Audit
- No illegal H2/H3 in Veil  
- Neon romance arcs fit the heat ladder  
- Loom arcs hit convergence correctly  
- Seraphine/Lucien/Caro/Elisabet rungs sync

### Mechanica Sanity Check
- ActionState rhythms  
- No illegal boosts  
- No VT/LT contamination at wrong times

This gives us a **structurally perfect episode skeleton**.

---

## **PHASE 4 — Detailed Beat Expansion (episode-level beats)**
NOW we begin expanding episode beats.

Every episode gets:

- ECID  
- 3–7 beats  
- Beat IDs  
- Symbol + motif mapping  
- UARS cost  
- Heat rung (if applicable)  
- Resonance-state tag  
- Character motion  
- Plot motion  
- Emotional/spiritual motion  
- Antagonist vector  
- VFX ceiling  
- Scene entry/exit logic  

This is where **project memory becomes fully utilized**.

Because now we know exactly:

- Where each character is in their emotional arc  
- What their resonance capacity is  
- What the corridor/weather rules allow  
- What romance/heat rungs permit  
- What antagonists can pressure  
- What symbols can appear safely  
- What VFX escalation is allowed  
- Where FUN beats must land  
- How pacing should rise/fall within each act

This is the moment detailed beats are **safe to write**.

---

## **PHASE 5 — Full Memory-Based Audit**
Once detailed beats exist:

- Mechanica v4 audit  
- Symbol/Motif audit  
- Resonance logic audit  
- Antagonist-pressure audit  
- Emotional circuit audit  
- FUN audit  
- Romance ladder audit  
- POV voice audit  

This is the second full audit — the deep audit — which ensures no beat violates any canon.

---

# 🎯 **SO WHAT’S THE OPTIMAL PATH?**
Exactly this:

### **1. Build full placeholder episode grid**  ← Next step  
### **2. Place act-level beats into that grid**  
### **3. Structural Audit using project memory**  
### **4. THEN expand detailed beats**  
### **5. Final audits**  

This gives you:

- Minimal rewriting  
- Maximum continuity  
- Zero drift  
- Full integration with every memory item
